It enables finding bugs in Python code through intelligent mutation-based testing.\n\n## Installation\n\n```bash\npip install atheris\n```\n\n## Basic Atheris Fuzzer Structure\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport sys\n\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Main fuzzing function that receives mutated input.\n\n    Args:\n        data: bytes object containing fuzz input\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        # Convert bytes to appropriate format\n        fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n        # Example: Extract different data types\n        string_input = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(100)\n        int_input = fdp.ConsumeInt(4)\n\n        # Call target function with fuzzed data\n        # target_function(string_input, int_input)\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        # Expected exceptions should be caught\n        # Unexpected crashes will be reported by fuzzer\n        pass\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n## FuzzedDataProvider Methods\n\nThe `FuzzedDataProvider` class helps consume bytes in structured ways:\n\n- `ConsumeBytes(count)`: Get raw bytes\n- `ConsumeUnicode(count)`: Get Unicode string (may include surrogates)\n- `ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(count)`: Get valid Unicode string\n- `ConsumeString(count)`: Get ASCII string\n- `ConsumeInt(bytes)`: Get integer (1, 2, 4, or 8 bytes)\n- `ConsumeIntInRange(min, max)`: Get bounded integer\n- `ConsumeFloat()`: Get float value\n- `ConsumeBool()`: Get boolean value\n- `ConsumeIntList(count, bytes)`: Get list of integers\n- `PickValueInList(list)`: Choose from list\n- `ConsumeRemainingBytes()`: Get all remaining bytes\n\n## Running the Fuzzer\n\n```bash\n# Run fuzzer for limited time\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10\n\n# Run with specific number of runs\npython fuzz.py -runs=1000\n\n# Use corpus directory\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=10\n\n# Set memory limit\npython fuzz.py -rss_limit_mb=2048\n```\n\n## Common libFuzzer Options\n\n- `-max_total_time=N`: Run for N seconds\n- `-runs=N`: Execute N iterations\n- `-max_len=N`: Maximum input length\n- `-dict=file`: Use dictionary for mutations\n- `-jobs=N`: Run N fuzzing jobs in parallel\n- `-workers=N`: Use N worker processes\n- `-timeout=N`: Timeout per test case\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Keep TestOneInput Fast**: Fuzzer needs to execute many iterations\n2. **Handle Expected Exceptions**: Catch exceptions that are normal behavior\n3. **Let Real Bugs Crash**: Don't catch unexpected errors\n4. **Use Structured Input**: FuzzedDataProvider helps create valid inputs\n5. **Seed Corpus**: Provide example inputs to guide fuzzing\n6. **Validate Assumptions**: Add assertions for invariants\n\n## Example: Fuzzing JSON Parser\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport sys\nimport json\n\n@atheris.instrument_func\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n    json_string = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(1000)\n\n    try:\n        parsed = json.loads(json_string)\n        # Test round-trip\n        json.dumps(parsed)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError, TypeError):\n        # Expected errors\n        pass\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n## Instrumentation\n\nUse `@atheris.instrument_func` decorator to instrument functions for better coverage tracking:\n\n```python\n@atheris.instrument_func\ndef my_function(x):\n    return x * 2\n```\n\nFor entire modules:\n```python\natheris.instrument_all()\n```\n\n## Timeout Handling\n\n```python\nimport signal\n\ndef timeout_handler(signum, frame):\n    raise TimeoutError(\"Operation timed out\")\n\n@atheris.instrument_func\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    signal.signal(signal.SIGALRM, timeout_handler)\n    signal.alarm(1)  # 1 second timeout\n    try:\n        # Fuzz target\n        pass\n    except TimeoutError:\n        pass\n    finally:\n        signal.alarm(0)\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":147,"cache_read_input_tokens":19485,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":147,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a936f5e9-73c2-4df3-ad1d-a672e1b44ee1","uuid":"11096650-dc58-4bf8-a70f-34c79fb9c3ec"}

venv (Built-in, Python 3.3+)\n\n```bash\n# Create virtual environment\npython3 -m venv /path/to/venv\n\n# Activate (Linux/Mac)\nsource /path/to/venv/bin/activate\n\n# Activate (Windows)\n/path/to/venv/Scripts/activate\n\n# Deactivate\ndeactivate\n```\n\n### Using virtualenv\n\n```bash\n# Install\npip install virtualenv\n\n# Create environment\nvirtualenv /path/to/venv\n\n# With specific Python version\nvirtualenv -p python3.9 /path/to/venv\n```\n\n## Installing Dependencies\n\n### From requirements.txt\n\n```bash\n# Activate environment first\nsource /path/to/venv/bin/activate\n\n# Install requirements\npip install -r requirements.txt\n\n# Install in development mode\npip install -e .\n```\n\n### From pyproject.toml\n\n```bash\n# Modern Python projects use pyproject.toml\npip install .\n\n# With optional dependencies\npip install .[dev]\npip install .[test]\npip install .[all]\n\n# In editable mode\npip install -e .\npip install -e .[dev]\n```\n\n### From setup.py\n\n```bash\n# Legacy projects\npip install .\n\n# Development mode\npip install -e .\n```\n\n## Running Commands in Virtual Environment\n\n### Without Activating\n\n```bash\n# Execute Python script\n/path/to/venv/bin/python script.py\n\n# Run pip\n/path/to/venv/bin/pip install package\n\n# Run installed command\n/path/to/venv/bin/command\n```\n\n### With Activation\n\n```bash\nsource /path/to/venv/bin/activate\npython script.py\npip install package\ndeactivate\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **One Environment Per Project**: Keep dependencies isolated\n2. **Don't Commit .venv**: Add to .gitignore\n3. **Pin Dependencies**: Use requirements.txt or poetry.lock\n4. **Use Editable Installs**: `pip install -e .` for development\n5. **Recreate if Broken**: Delete and recreate rather than fix\n\n## Common Directory Structures\n\n```\nproject/\n├── .venv/              # Virtual environment (gitignored)\n├── src/\n│   └── mypackage/\n├── tests/\n├── requirements.txt    # Dependencies\n├── setup.py           # Package setup\n└── pyproject.toml     # Modern config\n```\n\n## Checking Active Environment\n\n```bash\n# Show Python location\nwhich python\n\n# Show pip location\nwhich pip\n\n# List installed packages\npip list\n\n# Show environment info\npip show package_name\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Wrong Python Version\n```bash\n# Specify Python explicitly\npython3.9 -m venv .venv\n```\n\n### Broken Environment\n```bash\n# Delete and recreate\nrm -rf .venv\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### Permission Issues\n```bash\n# Don't use sudo with virtual environments\n# Create in user-writable location\n```\n\n## Alternative Explore Directory Structure\n\n```bash\n# Find main package directories\nfind . -type f -name \"__init__.py\" | head -10\n\n# Identify main source directory\nls -la src/ 2>/dev/null || ls -la */\n\n# Find all Python files\nfind . -name \"*.py\" -not -path \"./.venv/*\" -not -path \"*/tests/*\"\n```\n\n### 2. Read Package Configuration\n\n#### pyproject.toml (Modern)\n```toml\n[project]\nname = \"package-name\"\nversion = \"1.0.0\"\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\"]\n```\n\n#### setup.py (Legacy)\n```python\nsetup(\n    name=\"package-name\",\n    packages=find_packages(),\n    install_requires=[...],\n)\n```\n\n### 3. Identify Main Modules\n\nLook for:\n- `__init__.py`: Package entry points\n- `__main__.py`: CLI entry points\n- Core modules in src/ or package root\n- API files (often named `api.py`, `core.py`, `main.py`)\n\n## Finding Functions to Test\n\n### High-Value Targets\n\n1. **Parsing Functions**: Input validation, format conversion\n   - JSON/XML/YAML parsers\n   - Configuration readers\n   - Data deserializers\n\n2. **String Processing**: Text manipulation, encoding\n   - Template engines\n   - String formatters\n   - Encoders/decoders\n\n3. **Data Structures**: Complex data handling\n   - Custom collections\n   - Tree/graph operations\n   - Data transformations\n\n4. **File I/O**: File reading/writing\n   - File parsers\n   - Format converters\n   - Archive handlers\n\n5. **Network/API**: External input handling\n   - HTTP request parsing\n   - URL processing\n   - Protocol implementations\n\n6. **Mathematical Operations**: Numerical computation\n   - Calculations with edge cases\n   - Type conversions\n   - Range operations\n\n### Analysis Checklist\n\n```markdown\n## Library Analysis: [Library Name]\n\n### Main Package Structure\n- Main module: path/to/main\n- Sub-packages: pkg1, pkg2, pkg3\n\n### Key Public APIs\n1. Function: `module.function_name(args)`\n   - Purpose: What it does\n   - Input types: str, int, dict, etc.\n   - Potential issues: parsing, validation, edge cases\n\n2. Class: `module.ClassName`\n   - Methods: method1, method2\n   - Input handling: initialization, setters\n\n### Functions for Fuzzing Priority\n\n#### High Priority\n- Complex parsing functions\n- Input validation routines\n- Data transformation functions\n\n#### Medium Priority\n- Utility functions with string inputs\n- Configuration handlers\n- Format converters\n\n#### Low Priority\n- Simple getters/setters\n- Pure mathematical operations\n- Well-tested stdlib wrappers\n\n### Dependencies to Install\n- Required: pkg1, pkg2\n- Optional: pkg3 (for feature X)\n\n### Testing Notes\n- Edge cases: empty input, very large input, special characters\n- Expected exceptions: ValueError, TypeError, ParseError\n- Invariants: output should satisfy X property\n```\n\n## Code Exploration Patterns\n\n### Find Entry Points\n\n```python\n# Read __init__.py for public API\nwith open(\"package/__init__.py\") as f:\n    # Look for __all__ list\n    # Look for imports from submodules\n```\n\n### Find Complex Functions\n\n```bash\n# Find functions with many parameters\ngrep -r \"^def.*(.*, .*, .*, .*)\" --include=\"*.py\"\n\n# Find error handling (might indicate complex logic)\ngrep -r \"try:\" --include=\"*.py\" | wc -l\n\n# Find type hints (modern, well-structured code)\ngrep -r \"def.*->.*:\" --include=\"*.py\"\n```\n\n### Check Documentation\n\n```bash\n# Find docstrings\ngrep -r '\"\"\"' --include=\"*.py\"\n\n# Look for examples\nfind . -name \"examples\" -o -name \"docs\"\n\n# Check README\ncat README.md README.rst 2>/dev/null\n```\n\n## Example Analysis Template\n\n```markdown\n# Testing Notes: [Library Name]\n\n## Overview\nBrief description of what the library does.\n\n## Main Entry Points\n- `module.main_function()`: Primary API\n- `module.Class`: Main class interface\n\n## Functions to Fuzz\n\n### 1. module.parse_input(data: str)\n- **Purpose**: Parses input data into structured format\n- **Input**: String data in specific format\n- **Output**: Parsed object/dict\n- **Why fuzz**: Complex parsing logic, many edge cases\n- **Approach**: Feed random strings, check for crashes\n\n### 2. module.transform(obj: dict, schema: dict)\n- **Purpose**: Transforms data according to schema\n- **Input**: Dictionary and schema specification\n- **Output**: Transformed data\n- **Why fuzz**: Schema validation, type conversion\n- **Approach**: Generate random dicts and schemas\n\n## Expected Exceptions\n- `ParseError`: Invalid input format\n- `ValidationError`: Schema mismatch\n- `ValueError`: Invalid values\n\n## Edge Cases to Consider\n- Empty strings/dicts\n- Very large inputs (MB+ data)\n- Unicode/special characters\n- Nested structures\n- Circular references\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":147,"cache_read_input_tokens":19485,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":147,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a936f5e9-73c2-4df3-ad1d-a672e1b44ee1","uuid":"4c33aaf8-362c-4bb2-aebc-67c4fff7daee"}

Understanding pyproject.toml\n\n## Overview\n`pyproject.toml` is the modern Python project configuration file (PEP 518, 621) that centralizes build system requirements, project metadata, and tool configurations.\n\n## Basic Structure\n\n```toml\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"setuptools\", \"wheel\"]\nbuild-backend = \"setuptools.build_meta\"\n\n[project]\nname = \"my-package\"\nversion = \"1.0.0\"\ndescription = \"A sample package\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.7\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"requests>=2.20\",\n    \"numpy>=1.19\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"setuptools_scm\"]\nbuild-backend = \"setuptools.build_meta\"\n```\n\nCommon build backends:\n- `setuptools.build_meta`: Traditional setuptools\n- `poetry.core.masonry.api`: Poetry\n- `flit_core.buildapi`: Flit\n- `hatchling.build`: Hatch\n\n### [project]\nCore project metadata:\n```toml\n[project]\nname = \"package-name\"\nversion = \"1.0.0\"\ndescription = \"Short description\"\nreadme = \"README.md\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.8\"\nlicense = {text = \"MIT\"}\nauthors = [\n    {name = \"Author Name\", email = \"author@example.com\"}\n]\nkeywords = [\"keyword1\", \"keyword2\"]\nclassifiers = [\n    \"Development Status :: 4 - Beta\",\n    \"Programming Language :: Python :: 3\",\n]\n```\n\n### Dependencies\n\n#### Core Dependencies\n```toml\n[project]\ndependencies = [\n    \"requests>=2.28.0\",\n    \"numpy>=1.20,<2.0\",\n    \"pandas\",\n]\n```\n\n#### Optional Dependencies\n```toml\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\n    \"pytest>=7.0\",\n    \"black==22.10.0\",\n    \"mypy\",\n]\ntest = [\"pytest\", \"coverage\"]\ndocs = [\"sphinx\", \"sphinx-rtd-theme\"]\nall = [\"my-package[dev,test,docs]\"]\n```\n\nInstall with: `pip install .[dev]` or `pip install .[all]`\n\n### [project.scripts]\nDefine command-line entry points:\n```toml\n[project.scripts]\nmy-command = \"my_package.cli:main\"\nanother-tool = \"my_package.tools:run\"\n```\n\n### [project.entry-points]\nPlugin entry [tool.poetry]\nPoetry-specific:\n```toml\n[tool.poetry]\nname = \"my-package\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\n\n[tool.poetry.dependencies]\npython = \"^3.8\"\nrequests = \"^2.28\"\n\n[tool.poetry.dev-dependencies]\npytest = \"^7.0\"\n```\n\n### [tool.black]\nBlack formatter config:\n```toml\n[tool.black]\nline-length = 88\ntarget-version = ['py38']\ninclude = Atheris (Python fuzzing) uses libFuzzer's engine, so the same options apply.\n\n## Basic Usage\n\n```bash\n# Run fuzzer\npython fuzz.py\n\n# With options\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=60 -max_len=1024\n\n# With corpus directory\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=60\n\n# Multiple corpus directories (merge)\npython fuzz.py corpus1/ corpus2/ -max_total_time=60\n```\n\n## Essential Options\n\n### Time and Iteration Control\n\n```bash\n# Run for N seconds\n-max_total_time=N\n\n# Run for N iterations\n-runs=N\n\n# Run indefinitely (default)\n# (no option needed)\n\n# Timeout per test input (seconds)\n-timeout=N  # Default: 1200 (20 minutes)\n```\n\n### Input Control\n\n```bash\n# Maximum length of test input (bytes)\n-max_len=N  # Default: auto-grows\n\n# Minimum length of test input\n-len_control=0  # Disable length control\n\n# Exact length\n-max_len=N -len_control=0\n```\n\n### Corpus Management\n\n```bash\n# Use seed corpus\npython fuzz.py corpus/\n\n# Write interesting inputs to corpus\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=60\n# New inputs automatically saved to corpus/\n\n# Merge multiple corpora\npython fuzz.py -merge=1 new_corpus/ corpus1/ corpus2/\n\n# Minimize corpus (keep smallest inputs with same coverage)\npython fuzz.py -merge=1 -merge_control_file=corpus.txt minimized_corpus/ full_corpus/\n```\n\n### Output and Logging\n\n```bash\n# Print stats every N seconds\n-print_final_stats=1  # Print at end\n\n# Verbosity level (0-2)\n-verbosity=1  # Default: 1\n\n# Suppress output\n-close_fd_mask=3  # Close stdout and stderr\n\n# Redirect to file (shell redirect)\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10 2>&1 | tee fuzz.log\n```\n\n### Resource Limits\n\n```bash\n# Memory limit (MB)\n-rss_limit_mb=N  # Default: 2048\n\n# Error exit code\n-error_exitcode=N  # Default: 77\n\n# Out of memory exit code\n-oom_exitcode=N  # Default: 71\n```\n\n### Parallel Fuzzing\n\n```bash\n# Number of parallel jobs\n-jobs=N  # Runs N independent fuzzing processes\n\n# Number of workers\n-workers=N  # Similar to jobs\n\n# Example: 4 parallel processes, 60 seconds each\npython fuzz.py -jobs=4 -max_total_time=60 corpus/\n```\n\n## Advanced Options\n\n### Mutation Control\n\n```bash\n# Use mutation dictionary\n-dict=dictionary.txt\n\n# Cross-over with corpus\n-cross_over=1  # Default: 1 (enabled)\n\n# Mutation depth\n-mutate_depth=N  # Default: 5\n\n# Entropy\n-use_value_profile=1  # Use value profiling\n```\n\n### Coverage and Features\n\n```bash\n# Use counters for coverage\n-use_counters=1  # Default: 1\n\n# Track indirect calls\n-use_cmp=1  # Default: 1\n\n# Data flow tracing\n-data_flow_trace=1\n```\n\n### Determinism\n\n```bash\n# Random seed\n-seed=N  # For reproducibility\n\n# Deterministic mode\n-deterministic_mode=1\n```\n\n### Crash Handling\n\n```bash\n# Minimize crash input\npython fuzz.py -minimize_crash=1 crash-file\n\n# Cleanse crash (simplify while keeping crash)\npython fuzz.py -cleanse_crash=1 crash-file\n\n# Exact artifact path\n-exact_artifact_path=path/to/crash\n```\n\n### Dictionary Format\n\n```text\n# dict.txt\n# Comments start with #\n\n# Strings\n\"keyword\"\n\"important_value\"\n\n# Hex values\n\"\\x00\\xff\"\n\n# With level (nesting depth)\n\"outer\" @1\n\"inner\" @2\n```\n\nUsage: `-dict=dict.txt`\n\n## Common Workflows\n\n### Quick Validation (10 seconds)\n\n```bash\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10 -max_len=1024\n```\n\n### Continuous Fuzzing (with corpus)\n\n```bash\n# Initial run\nmkdir corpus\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=3600\n\n# Continue fuzzing (reuses corpus)\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=3600\n```\n\n### Parallel Fuzzing\n\n```bash\n# 8 parallel jobs, 1 hour each\npython fuzz.py -jobs=8 -max_total_time=3600 corpus/\n```\n\n### Reproduce Crash\n\n```bash\n# Run single input\npython fuzz.py crash-sample\n\n# Minimize crash\npython fuzz.py -minimize_crash=1 crash-sample\n```\n\n### Merge Corpora\n\n```bash\n# Merge and minimize\npython fuzz.py -merge=1 merged_corpus/ corpus1/ corpus2/ corpus3/\n```\n\n## Environment Variables\n\n```bash\n# AFL-compatible\nexport AFL_SKIP_CRASHES=1\n\n# Sanitizer options\nexport ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=0:allocator_may_return_null=1\nexport UBSAN_OPTIONS=print_stacktrace=1\n```\n\n## Output Interpretation\n\n```\n#2      INITED cov: 15 ft: 16 corp: 1/1b exec/s: 0 rss: 25Mb\n#1024   NEW    cov: 45 ft: 87 corp: 23/156b exec/s: 512 rss: 27Mb\n```\n\n- `#N`: Iteration number\n- `cov`: Coverage (edges/blocks)\n- `ft`: Features (coverage counters)\n- `corp`: Corpus size (count/total bytes)\n- `exec/s`: Executions per second\n- `rss`: Memory usage\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n- `0`: No issues found (reached iteration/time limit)\n- `1`: Crash found\n- `77`: Timeout (default `-error_exitcode`)\n- `71`: Out of memory (default `-oom_exitcode`)\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Start Short**: Use `-max_total_time=10` for It enables finding bugs in Python code through intelligent mutation-based testing.\n\n## Installation\n\n```bash\npip install atheris\n```\n\n## Basic Atheris Fuzzer Structure\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport sys\n\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Main fuzzing function that receives mutated input.\n\n    Args:\n        data: bytes object containing fuzz input\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        # Convert bytes to appropriate format\n        fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n        # Example: Extract different data types\n        string_input = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(100)\n        int_input = fdp.ConsumeInt(4)\n\n        # Call target function with fuzzed data\n        # target_function(string_input, int_input)\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        # Expected exceptions should be caught\n        # Unexpected crashes will be reported by fuzzer\n        pass\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n## FuzzedDataProvider Methods\n\nThe `FuzzedDataProvider` class helps consume bytes in structured ways:\n\n- `ConsumeBytes(count)`: Get raw bytes\n- `ConsumeUnicode(count)`: Get Unicode string (may include surrogates)\n- `ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(count)`: Get valid Unicode string\n- `ConsumeString(count)`: Get ASCII string\n- `ConsumeInt(bytes)`: Get integer (1, 2, 4, or 8 bytes)\n- `ConsumeIntInRange(min, max)`: Get bounded integer\n- `ConsumeFloat()`: Get float value\n- `ConsumeBool()`: Get boolean value\n- `ConsumeIntList(count, bytes)`: Get list of integers\n- `PickValueInList(list)`: Choose from list\n- `ConsumeRemainingBytes()`: Get all remaining bytes\n\n## Running the Fuzzer\n\n```bash\n# Run fuzzer for limited time\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10\n\n# Run with specific number of runs\npython fuzz.py -runs=1000\n\n# Use corpus directory\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=10\n\n# Set memory limit\npython fuzz.py -rss_limit_mb=2048\n```\n\n## Common libFuzzer Options\n\n- `-max_total_time=N`: Run for N seconds\n- `-runs=N`: Execute N iterations\n- `-max_len=N`: Maximum input length\n- `-dict=file`: Use dictionary for mutations\n- `-jobs=N`: Run N fuzzing jobs in parallel\n- `-workers=N`: Use N worker processes\n- `-timeout=N`: Timeout per test case\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Keep TestOneInput Fast**: Fuzzer needs to execute many iterations\n2. **Handle Expected Exceptions**: Catch exceptions that are normal behavior\n3. **Let Real Bugs Crash**: Don't catch unexpected errors\n4. **Use Structured Input**: FuzzedDataProvider helps create valid inputs\n5. **Seed Corpus**: Provide example inputs to guide fuzzing\n6. **Validate Assumptions**: Add assertions for invariants\n\n## Example: Fuzzing JSON Parser\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport sys\nimport json\n\n@atheris.instrument_func\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n    json_string = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(1000)\n\n    try:\n        parsed = json.loads(json_string)\n        # Test round-trip\n        json.dumps(parsed)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError, TypeError):\n        # Expected errors\n        pass\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n## Instrumentation\n\nUse `@atheris.instrument_func` decorator to instrument functions for better coverage tracking:\n\n```python\n@atheris.instrument_func\ndef my_function(x):\n    return x * 2\n```\n\nFor entire modules:\n```python\natheris.instrument_all()\n```\n\n## Timeout Handling\n\n```python\nimport signal\n\ndef timeout_handler(signum, frame):\n    raise TimeoutError(\"Operation timed out\")\n\n@atheris.instrument_func\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    signal.signal(signal.SIGALRM, timeout_handler)\n    signal.alarm(1)  # 1 second timeout\n    try:\n        # Fuzz target\n        pass\n    except TimeoutError:\n        pass\n    finally:\n        signal.alarm(0)\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

projects use pyproject.toml\npip install .\n\n# With optional dependencies\npip install .[dev]\npip install .[test]\npip install .[all]\n\n# In editable mode\npip install -e .\npip install -e With Activation\n\n```bash\nsource /path/to/venv/bin/activate\npython script.py\npip install package\ndeactivate\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **One Environment Per Project**: Keep dependencies isolated\n2. **Don't Commit .venv**: Add to .gitignore\n3. **Pin Dependencies**: Use requirements.txt or poetry.lock\n4. **Use Editable Installs**: `pip install -e .` for development\n5. **Recreate if Explore Directory Structure\n\n```bash\n# Find main package directories\nfind . -type f -name \"__init__.py\" | head -10\n\n# Identify main source directory\nls -la src/ 2>/dev/null || ls -la */\n\n# Find all Python files\nfind . -name \"*.py\" -not -path \"./.venv/*\" -not -path \"*/tests/*\"\n```\n\n### 2. Read Package Configuration\n\n#### pyproject.toml (Modern)\n```toml\n[project]\nname = \"package-name\"\nversion = \"1.0.0\"\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\"]\n```\n\n#### setup.py (Legacy)\n```python\nsetup(\n    name=\"package-name\",\n    packages=find_packages(),\n    install_requires=[...],\n)\n```\n\n### 3. Identify Main Modules\n\nLook for:\n- `__init__.py`: Package entry points\n- `__main__.py`: CLI entry points\n- Core modules in src/ or package root\n- API files (often named `api.py`, `core.py`, `main.py`)\n\n## Finding Functions to Test\n\n### High-Value Targets\n\n1. **Parsing Functions**: Input validation, format conversion\n   - JSON/XML/YAML parsers\n   - Configuration readers\n   - Data deserializers\n\n2. **String Processing**: Text manipulation, encoding\n   - Template engines\n   - String formatters\n   - Encoders/decoders\n\n3. **Data Structures**: Complex data handling\n   - Custom collections\n   - Tree/graph operations\n   - Data transformations\n\n4. **File I/O**: File reading/writing\n   - File parsers\n   - Format converters\n   - Archive handlers\n\n5. **Network/API**: External input handling\n   - HTTP request parsing\n   - URL processing\n   - Protocol implementations\n\n6. **Mathematical Operations**: Numerical computation\n   - Calculations with edge cases\n   - Type conversions\n   - Range operations\n\n### Analysis Checklist\n\n```markdown\n## Library Analysis: [Library Name]\n\n### Main Package Structure\n- Main module: path/to/main\n- Sub-packages: pkg1, pkg2, pkg3\n\n### Key Public APIs\n1. Function: `module.function_name(args)`\n   - Purpose: What it does\n   - Input types: str, int, dict, etc.\n   - Potential issues: parsing, validation, edge cases\n\n2. Class: `module.ClassName`\n   - Methods: method1, method2\n   - Input handling: initialization, setters\n\n### Functions for Fuzzing Priority\n\n#### High Priority\n- Complex parsing functions\n- Input validation routines\n- Data transformation functions\n\n#### Medium Priority\n- Utility functions with string inputs\n- Configuration handlers\n- Format converters\n\n#### Low Priority\n- Simple getters/setters\n- Pure mathematical operations\n- Well-tested stdlib wrappers\n\n### Dependencies to Install\n- Required: pkg1, pkg2\n- Optional: pkg3 (for feature X)\n\n### Testing Notes\n- Edge cases: empty input, very large input, special characters\n- Expected exceptions: ValueError, TypeError, ParseError\n- Invariants: output should satisfy X property\n```\n\n## Code Exploration Patterns\n\n### Find Entry Points\n\n```python\n# Read __init__.py for public API\nwith open(\"package/__init__.py\") as f:\n    # Look for __all__ list\n    # Look for imports from submodules\n```\n\n### Find Complex Functions\n\n```bash\n# Find functions with many parameters\ngrep -r \"^def.*(.*, .*, .*, .*)\" --include=\"*.py\"\n\n# Find error handling (might indicate complex logic)\ngrep -r \"try:\" --include=\"*.py\" | wc -l\n\n# Find type hints (modern, well-structured code)\ngrep -r \"def.*->.*:\" --include=\"*.py\"\n```\n\n### Check Documentation\n\n```bash\n# Find docstrings\ngrep -r '\"\"\"' --include=\"*.py\"\n\n# Look for examples\nfind . -name \"examples\" -o -name \"docs\"\n\n# Check README\ncat README.md README.rst 2>/dev/null\n```\n\n## Example Analysis Template\n\n```markdown\n# Testing Notes: [Library Name]\n\n## Overview\nBrief description of what the library does.\n\n## Main Entry Points\n- `module.main_function()`: Primary API\n- `module.Class`: Main class interface\n\n## Functions to Fuzz\n\n### 1. module.parse_input(data: str)\n- **Purpose**: Parses input data into structured format\n- **Input**: String data in specific format\n- **Output**: Parsed object/dict\n- **Why fuzz**: Complex parsing logic, many edge cases\n- **Approach**: Feed random strings, check for crashes\n\n### 2. module.transform(obj: dict, schema: dict)\n- **Purpose**: Transforms data according to schema\n- **Input**: Dictionary and schema specification\n- **Output**: Transformed data\n- **Why fuzz**: Schema validation, type conversion\n- **Approach**: Generate random dicts and schemas\n\n## Expected Exceptions\n- `ParseError`: Invalid input format\n- `ValidationError`: Schema mismatch\n- `ValueError`: Invalid values\n\n## Edge Cases to Consider\n- Empty strings/dicts\n- Very large inputs (MB+ data)\n- Unicode/special characters\n- Nested structures\n- Circular references\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Understanding pyproject.toml\n\n## Overview\n`pyproject.toml` is the modern Python project configuration file (PEP 518, 621) that centralizes build system requirements, project metadata, and tool configurations.\n\n## Basic Structure\n\n```toml\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"setuptools\", \"wheel\"]\nbuild-backend = \"setuptools.build_meta\"\n\n[project]\nname = \"my-package\"\nversion = \"1.0.0\"\ndescription = \"A sample package\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.7\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"requests>=2.20\",\n    \"numpy>=1.19\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\", \"mypy\"]\ndocs = [\"sphinx\"]\n```\n\n## Key Sections\n\n### [build-system]\nSpecifies build requirements:\n```toml\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"setuptools>=45\", \"wheel\", \"setuptools_scm\"]\nbuild-backend = \"setuptools.build_meta\"\n```\n\nCommon build backends:\n- `setuptools.build_meta`: Traditional setuptools\n- `poetry.core.masonry.api`: Poetry\n- `flit_core.buildapi`: Flit\n- `hatchling.build`: Hatch\n\n### [project]\nCore project metadata:\n```toml\n[project]\nname = \"package-name\"\nversion = \"1.0.0\"\ndescription = \"Short description\"\nreadme = \"README.md\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.8\"\nlicense = {text = \"MIT\"}\nauthors = [\n    {name = \"Author Name\", email = \"author@example.com\"}\n]\nkeywords = [\"keyword1\", \"keyword2\"]\nclassifiers = [\n    \"Development Status :: 4 - Beta\",\n    \"Programming Language :: Python :: 3\",\n]\n```\n\n### Dependencies\n\n#### Core Dependencies\n```toml\n[project]\ndependencies = [\n    \"requests>=2.28.0\",\n    \"numpy>=1.20,<2.0\",\n    \"pandas\",\n]\n```\n\n#### Optional Dependencies\n```toml\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\n    \"pytest>=7.0\",\n    \"black==22.10.0\",\n    \"mypy\",\n]\ntest = [\"pytest\", \"coverage\"]\ndocs = [\"sphinx\", \"sphinx-rtd-theme\"]\nall = [\"my-package[dev,test,docs]\"]\n```\n\nInstall with: `pip install .[dev]` or `pip install .[all]`\n\n### [project.scripts]\nDefine command-line entry points:\n```toml\n[project.scripts]\nmy-command = \"my_package.cli:main\"\nanother-tool = \"my_package.tools:run\"\n```\n\n### [project.entry-points]\nPlugin entry points:\n```toml\n[project.entry-points.\"some.plugin.namespace\"]\nmy-plugin = \"my_package.plugins:MyPlugin\"\n```\n\n## Tool-Specific Sections\n\n### [tool.setuptools]\nSetuptools configuration:\n```toml\n[tool.setuptools]\npackages = [\"my_package\", \"my_package.subpkg\"]\n\n[tool.setuptools.package-data]\nmy_package = [\"*.txt\", \"data/*.json\"]\n```\n\n### [tool.poetry]\nPoetry-specific:\n```toml\n[tool.poetry]\nname = \"my-package\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\n\n[tool.poetry.dependencies]\npython = \"^3.8\"\nrequests = \"^2.28\"\n\n[tool.poetry.dev-dependencies]\npytest = \"^7.0\"\n```\n\n### [tool.black]\nBlack formatter config:\n```toml\n[tool.black]\nline-length = 88\ntarget-version = ['py38']\ninclude = '\\.pyi?$'\n```\n\n### [tool.pytest.ini_options]\nPytest configuration:\n```toml\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\npython_files = \"test_*.py\"\naddopts = \"-v --cov=my_package\"\n```\n\n### [tool.mypy]\nType checking:\n```toml\n[tool.mypy]\npython_version = \"3.8\"\nwarn_return_any = true\nstrict_optional = true\n```\n\n## Reading pyproject.toml in Python\n\n```python\nimport tomli  # Python <3.11\n# or\nimport tomllib  # Python 3.11+\n\n# Read file\nwith open(\"pyproject.toml\", \"rb\") as f:\n    data = tomli.load(f)\n\n# Access data\nproject_name = data[\"project\"][\"name\"]\ndependencies = data[\"project\"][\"dependencies\"]\noptional_deps = data[\"project\"].get(\"optional-dependencies\", {})\n\n# Get all dependencies\nall_deps = dependencies.copy()\nfor group in optional_deps.values():\n    all_deps.extend(group)\n```\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### Determine Dependencies to Install\n\n```python\nimport tomli\n\nwith open(\"pyproject.toml\", \"rb\") as f:\n    config = tomli.load(f)\n\n# Get required dependencies\ndeps = config.get(\"project\", {}).get(\"dependencies\", [])\n\n# Get optional dev dependencies\ndev_deps = config.get(\"project\", {}).get(\"optional-dependencies\", {}).get(\"dev\", [])\n\n# Combine\nall_deps = deps + dev_deps\n```\n\n### Check Build Backend\n\n```toml\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"poetry-core>=1.0.0\"]\nbuild-backend = \"poetry.core.masonry.api\"\n```\n\nDifferent backends need different installation approaches:\n- Poetry: `poetry install`\n- Setuptools: `pip install -e .`\n- Flit: `flit install`\n\n## Installing from pyproject.toml\n\n```bash\n# Install package with dependencies\npip install .\n\n# Install in editable/development mode\npip install -e .\n\n# Install with optional dependencies\npip install -e .[dev]\npip install -e .[test,docs]\n\n# If using Poetry\npoetry install\n\n# If using Flit\nflit install\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Missing Build Dependencies\n```bash\n# Ensure pip is up to date\npip install --upgrade pip\n\n# Install build dependencies manually\npip install setuptools wheel\n```\n\n### Can't Find Package\nCheck `[tool.setuptools]` or package discovery:\n```toml\n[tool.setuptools]\npackages = [\"my_package\"]  # Explicit\n\n# Or\n[tool.setuptools.packages.find]\nwhere = [\"src\"]  # Look in src directory\n```\n\n### Version Issues\n```toml\n# Dynamic version from file\n[project]\ndynamic = [\"version\"]\n\n[tool.setuptools.dynamic]\nversion = {attr = \"my_package.__version__\"}\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Atheris (Python fuzzing) uses libFuzzer's engine, so the same options apply.\n\n## Basic Usage\n\n```bash\n# Run fuzzer\npython fuzz.py\n\n# With options\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=60 -max_len=1024\n\n# With corpus directory\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=60\n\n# Multiple corpus directories (merge)\npython fuzz.py corpus1/ corpus2/ -max_total_time=60\n```\n\n## Essential Options\n\n### Time and Iteration Control\n\n```bash\n# Run for N seconds\n-max_total_time=N\n\n# Run for N iterations\n-runs=N\n\n# Run indefinitely (default)\n# (no option needed)\n\n# Timeout per test input (seconds)\n-timeout=N  # Default: 1200 (20 minutes)\n```\n\n### Input Control\n\n```bash\n# Maximum length of test input (bytes)\n-max_len=N  # Default: auto-grows\n\n# Minimum length of test input\n-len_control=0  # Disable length control\n\n# Exact length\n-max_len=N -len_control=0\n```\n\n### Corpus Management\n\n```bash\n# Use seed corpus\npython fuzz.py corpus/\n\n# Write interesting inputs to corpus\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=60\n# New inputs automatically saved to corpus/\n\n# Merge multiple corpora\npython fuzz.py -merge=1 new_corpus/ corpus1/ corpus2/\n\n# Minimize corpus (keep smallest inputs with same coverage)\npython fuzz.py -merge=1 -merge_control_file=corpus.txt minimized_corpus/ full_corpus/\n```\n\n### Output and Logging\n\n```bash\n# Print stats every N seconds\n-print_final_stats=1  # Print at end\n\n# Verbosity level (0-2)\n-verbosity=1  # Default: 1\n\n# Suppress output\n-close_fd_mask=3  # Close stdout and stderr\n\n# Redirect to file (shell redirect)\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10 2>&1 | tee fuzz.log\n```\n\n### Resource Limits\n\n```bash\n# Memory limit (MB)\n-rss_limit_mb=N  # Default: 2048\n\n# Error exit code\n-error_exitcode=N  # Default: 77\n\n# Out of memory exit code\n-oom_exitcode=N  # Default: 71\n```\n\n### Parallel Fuzzing\n\n```bash\n# Number of parallel jobs\n-jobs=N  # Runs N independent fuzzing processes\n\n# Number of workers\n-workers=N  # Similar to jobs\n\n# Example: 4 parallel processes, 60 seconds each\npython fuzz.py -jobs=4 -max_total_time=60 corpus/\n```\n\n## Advanced Options\n\n### Mutation Control\n\n```bash\n# Use mutation dictionary\n-dict=dictionary.txt\n\n# Cross-over with corpus\n-cross_over=1  # Default: 1 (enabled)\n\n# Mutation depth\n-mutate_depth=N  # Default: 5\n\n# Entropy\n-use_value_profile=1  # Use value profiling\n```\n\n### Coverage and Features\n\n```bash\n# Use counters for coverage\n-use_counters=1  # Default: 1\n\n# Track indirect calls\n-use_cmp=1  # Default: 1\n\n# Data flow tracing\n-data_flow_trace=1\n```\n\n### Determinism\n\n```bash\n# Random seed\n-seed=N  # For reproducibility\n\n# Deterministic mode\n-deterministic_mode=1\n```\n\n### Crash Handling\n\n```bash\n# Minimize crash input\npython fuzz.py -minimize_crash=1 crash-file\n\n# Cleanse crash (simplify while keeping crash)\npython fuzz.py -cleanse_crash=1 crash-file\n\n# Exact artifact path\n-exact_artifact_path=path/to/crash\n```\n\n### Dictionary Format\n\n```text\n# dict.txt\n# Comments start with #\n\n# Strings\n\"keyword\"\n\"important_value\"\n\n# Hex values\n\"\\x00\\xff\"\n\n# With level (nesting depth)\n\"outer\" @1\n\"inner\" @2\n```\n\nUsage: `-dict=dict.txt`\n\n## Common Workflows\n\n### Quick Validation (10 seconds)\n\n```bash\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10 -max_len=1024\n```\n\n### Continuous Fuzzing (with corpus)\n\n```bash\n# Initial run\nmkdir corpus\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=3600\n\n# Continue fuzzing (reuses corpus)\npython fuzz.py corpus/ -max_total_time=3600\n```\n\n### Parallel Fuzzing\n\n```bash\n# 8 parallel jobs, 1 hour each\npython fuzz.py -jobs=8 -max_total_time=3600 corpus/\n```\n\n### Reproduce Crash\n\n```bash\n# Run single input\npython fuzz.py crash-sample\n\n# Minimize crash\npython fuzz.py -minimize_crash=1 crash-sample\n```\n\n### Merge Corpora\n\n```bash\n# Merge and minimize\npython fuzz.py -merge=1 merged_corpus/ corpus1/ corpus2/ corpus3/\n```\n\n## Environment Variables\n\n```bash\n# AFL-compatible\nexport AFL_SKIP_CRASHES=1\n\n# Sanitizer options\nexport ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=0:allocator_may_return_null=1\nexport UBSAN_OPTIONS=print_stacktrace=1\n```\n\n## Output Interpretation\n\n```\n#2      INITED cov: 15 ft: 16 corp: 1/1b exec/s: 0 rss: 25Mb\n#1024   NEW    cov: 45 ft: 87 corp: 23/156b exec/s: 512 rss: 27Mb\n```\n\n- `#N`: Iteration number\n- `cov`: Coverage (edges/blocks)\n- `ft`: Features (coverage counters)\n- `corp`: Corpus size (count/total bytes)\n- `exec/s`: Executions per second\n- `rss`: Memory usage\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n- `0`: No issues found (reached iteration/time limit)\n- `1`: Crash found\n- `77`: Timeout (default `-error_exitcode`)\n- `71`: Out of memory (default `-oom_exitcode`)\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Start Short**: Use `-max_total_time=10` for validation\n2. **Use Corpus**: Save interesting inputs for future runs\n3. **Set Memory Limit**: Prevent OOM with `-rss_limit_mb`\n4. **Parallel Fuzzing**: Use `-jobs` for faster coverage\n5. **Seed It processes user input, executes code, handles magic commands, and provides rich display capabilities.\n\n## Main Package Structure\n- Main module: IPython/\n- Core modules: core/interactiveshell.py, core/inputtransformer2.py, core/compilerop.py\n- Key functionality: Code execution, input transformation, magic commands, completion\n\n## Key Public APIs\n\n### 1. IPython.core.interactiveshell.InteractiveShell.run_cell(raw_cell)\n- **Location**: IPython/core/interactiveshell.py\n- **Purpose**: Execute a cell of code (main execution entry point)\n- **Input types**: String (Python code + IPython magic commands)\n- **Potential issues**: Malformed code, magic command parsing, special syntax\n- **Why fuzz**: Main API for code execution\n\n### 2. IPython.core.inputtransformer2.TransformerManager.transform_cell(cell)\n- **Location**: IPython/core/inputtransformer2.py\n- **Purpose**: Transform IPython syntax to valid Python\n- **Input types**: String (IPython-specific syntax)\n- **Potential issues**: Magic parsing, special operators (!, ?, %, etc.)\n\n### 3. IPython.core.compilerop.CachingCompiler\n- **Location**: IPython/core/compilerop.py\n- **Purpose**: Compile Python code with caching\n- **Input types**: String (Python source)\n- **Potential issues**: Syntax errors, edge cases in compilation\n\n### 4. IPython.core.completer.Completer.complete()\n- **Location**: IPython/core/completer.py\n- **Purpose**: Code completion\n- **Input types**: String (partial code)\n- **Potential issues**: Parsing incomplete code, attribute access\n\n## Functions for Fuzzing Priority\n\n### High Priority\n1. **run_cell(code)** - Main code execution\n   - Processes IPython magic commands (%magic, %%cellmagic)\n   - Handles special operators (!, ?, ??)\n   - Transforms and compiles code\n   - Highest complexity, most common use\n\n2. **transform_cell(cell)** - Input transformation\n   - Parses magic commands\n   - Handles line/cell magics\n   - Shell escape (!)\n   - Help operators (?, ??)\n\n3. **CachingCompiler** - Code compilation\n   - Compiles Python source\n   - Handles syntax errors\n   - Caching logic\n\n### Medium Priority\n1. **complete()** - Tab completion\n2. **prefilter()** - Input preprocessing\n3. **compile_ast()** - AST compilation\n\n## Dependencies to Install\n- Required: decorator, jedi, matplotlib-inline, prompt_toolkit, pygments,\n  stack_data, traitlets\n- Optional: pytest, ipykernel (for testing)\n\n## Testing Approach\n\n### Fuzz Target 1: transform_cell() with IPython syntax\n```python\n# Feed random IPython commands and syntax\nfrom IPython.core.inputtransformer2 import TransformerManager\ntm = TransformerManager()\ntm.transform_cell(fuzzed_ipython_code)\n```\n\n### Fuzz Target 2: Code compilation\n```python\n# Test compilation with malformed code\nfrom IPython.core.compilerop import CachingCompiler\ncompiler = CachingCompiler()\ncompiler(fuzzed_code, '<string>', 'exec')\n```\n\n### Expected Exceptions\n- `SyntaxError`: Invalid Python syntax\n- `IndentationError`: Invalid indentation\n- `ValueError`: Invalid operations\n- `UsageError`: Invalid magic command usage\n- `TokenError`: Tokenization errors\n\n## Edge Cases to Consider\n- Empty strings/cells\n- Magic commands: %time, %run, %%timeit, etc.\n- Shell escapes: !ls, !!python\n- Help operators: ?, ??\n- System assigns: var = !command\n- Multi-line magics\n- Nested quotes in magic args\n- Very long input lines\n- Unicode in code/magics\n- Incomplete code blocks\n- Mixed tabs/spaces\n- Cell separator (# %%)\n- IPython-specific syntax (!cmd, ?obj, %magic)\n- Async code (await without async def)\n- Invalid magic names\n- Special variables (_ , __, _i, _ih)\n\n## Fuzzing Strategy\nFocus on input transformation since IPython's unique value is handling:\n1. Magic commands (% and %%)\n2. Shell escapes (!)\n3. Help operators (?, ??)\n4. Special IPython syntax\n\nThe fuzzer should generate:\n- Valid Python code\n- IPython magic commands\n- Mixed Python + IPython syntax\n- Shell commands\n- Edge cases in parsing\n- Malformed magics\n- Special characters and operators\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2470,"cache_read_input_tokens":46241,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2470,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":26},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a936f5e9-73c2-4df3-ad1d-a672e1b44ee1","uuid":"2d93618a-eb4c-46e1-85e6-4530c19c2e98"}

It's designed as a drop-in replacement for the standard json module with better performance.\n\n## Main Package Structure\n- Main module: src/ujson/ (C extension)\n- Core files:\n  - python/ujson.c (Python bindings)\n  - python/objToJSON.c (Python object to JSON encoding)\n  - python/JSONtoObj.c (JSON to Python object decoding)\n  - lib/ultrajsonenc.c (core encoder)\n  - lib/ultrajsondec.c (core decoder)\n- Key functionality: JSON encoding/decoding\n\n## Key Public APIs\n\n### 1. ujson.loads(json_string, **kwargs)\n- **Purpose**: Decode JSON string to Python object\n- **Input types**: String (JSON data)\n- **Potential issues**: Malformed JSON, deeply nested structures, very large numbers, invalid escape sequences\n- **Why fuzz**: Main parsing API, handles untrusted input, C implementation with potential buffer issues\n\n### 2. ujson.dumps(obj, **kwargs)\n- **Purpose**: Encode Python object to JSON string\n- **Input types**: Python objects (dict, list, str, int, float, bool, None)\n- **Potential issues**: Circular references, non-serializable objects, encoding edge cases\n- **Why fuzz**: Main encoding API, complex object traversal\n\n### 3. ujson.load(fp, **kwargs)\n- **Purpose**: Decode JSON from file-like object\n- **Input types**: File object\n- **Potential issues**: Same as loads() plus file handling\n\n### 4. ujson.dump(obj, fp, **kwargs)\n- **Purpose**: Encode Python object to file\n- **Input types**: Python objects\n- **Potential issues**: Same as dumps() plus file handling\n\n## Functions for Fuzzing Priority\n\n### High Priority\n1. **ujson.loads(json_string)** - JSON parsing\n   - C implementation (higher risk for memory safety issues)\n   - Handles untrusted input\n   - Complex parsing logic\n   - Known history of security issues (per README warning)\n   - Edge cases: nested arrays/objects, large numbers, Unicode, escape sequences\n\n2. **ujson.dumps(obj)** - JSON encoding\n   - Object traversal\n   - Type handling\n   - String escaping\n   - Number formatting\n\n### Medium Priority\n1. **ujson.loads() with options** - Test various decoding options\n2. **ujson.dumps() with options** - Test encode_html_chars, ensure_ascii, etc.\n\n## Encoder Options to Test\n- `encode_html_chars`: Encode HTML special chars\n- `ensure_ascii`: Limit to ASCII output\n- `escape_forward_slashes`: Escape / characters\n- `sort_keys`: Sort dictionary keys\n- `indent`: Pretty-print indentation\n\n## Dependencies to Install\n- Build dependencies: setuptools, setuptools_scm\n- Runtime: None (C extension, no Python dependencies)\n- Note: Requires C compiler for building\n\n## Testing Approach\n\n### Fuzz Target 1: ujson.loads() - JSON parsing\n```python\nimport ujson\n# Feed random/malformed JSON strings\nujson.loads(fuzzed_json_string)\n```\n\n### Fuzz Target 2: ujson.dumps() - JSON encoding\n```python\nimport ujson\n# Feed random Python objects (dicts, lists, etc.)\nujson.dumps(fuzzed_python_object)\n```\n\n### Expected Exceptions\n- `ValueError`: Malformed JSON, invalid values\n- `OverflowError`: Numbers too large\n- `TypeError`: Non-serializable objects\n- `KeyError`: Invalid operations\n- Potential crashes: Buffer overflows, memory errors (C code)\n\n## Edge Cases to Consider\n\n### For loads() - Parsing\n- Empty strings\n- Very large JSON (MB+ size)\n- Deeply nested objects/arrays (1000+ levels)\n- Very long strings\n- Large numbers (beyond float64 range)\n- Special numbers: Infinity, -Infinity, NaN\n- Invalid escape sequences: \\x, \\u000, etc.\n- Incomplete escape sequences\n- Unicode characters: \\uXXXX, surrogate pairs\n- Null bytes in strings\n- Invalid JSON: trailing commas, missing quotes, etc.\n- Mixed valid/invalid JSON\n- Duplicate keys\n- Control characters in strings\n- Very long keys\n- Empty objects/arrays: {}, []\n\n### For dumps() - Encoding\n- Circular references: obj['self'] = obj\n- Non-serializable objects: functions, classes, modules\n- Large integers (beyond JSON spec)\n- Float special values: inf, -inf, nan\n- Unicode strings\n- Binary data\n- Empty containers\n- Nested structures\n- Custom objects\n- Dictionary with non-string keys\n- Very long strings/lists\n\n## Fuzzing Strategy\n**Primary Focus: ujson.loads()**\nThis is the highest priority target because:\n1. It's a C parser (memory safety risks)\n2. Handles untrusted external input\n3. Complex parsing logic\n4. README explicitly warns about security issues\n5. Maintenance-only mode means bugs may not be fixed quickly\n\nThe fuzzer should generate:\n- Valid JSON variations\n- Malformed JSON (syntax errors)\n- Edge case values (very large/small numbers)\n- Deep nesting\n- Long strings\n- Unicode and escape sequences\n- Boundary conditions\n\n**Secondary Focus: ujson.dumps()**\nTest encoding with:\n- Various Python object types\n- Edge case values\n- Complex nested structures\n- Special characters\n\n## Security Note\nFrom README: \"UltraJSON's architecture is fundamentally ill-suited to making changes without risk of introducing new security vulnerabilities.\" This makes it an excellent fuzzing target as:\n- Known history of vulnerabilities\n- C implementation prone to memory issues\n- Maintenance-only mode\n- Users encouraged to migrate away\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1984,"cache_read_input_tokens":59465,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1984,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a936f5e9-73c2-4df3-ad1d-a672e1b44ee1","uuid":"0f4178a8-df60-4db1-b147-79cb0e8752cd"}
